Now let me write QA-LS-4 (lines accumulate across clears) and QA-LS-8 (stat blocks update instantly). Let me also handle QA-LS-9 (baseline) which is already covered by baseline.sh.

For QA-LS-4 (lines counter accumulates): I can do multiple separate clears in a single session using the existing mechanism, but since each clear removes the prefilled rows, I need to re-prefill. Since I can't re-prefill without restarting, the simplest approach is to use `--debug-prefill-rows` with enough rows and drop multiple I pieces in sequence.

Actually, I can verify LNS accumulation by checking it after each clear using `--debug-prefill-rows 2` (2 rows) and one drop, then starting fresh with `--debug-init-lines 2` + `--debug-prefill-rows 1` for the second part.

But QA-LS-4 wants to verify "LINES increases by N in each clear and never decreases." Let me write a multi-session test that verifies this:

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_01X3z3EhXjzRN963ZTfRTUE5
```json
{
  "command": "cat > /Users/gabadi/workspace/gamaleds/tetris/.worktrees/QA/observation-harness/lines_and_score/las4_lines_accumulate.sh << 'SCRIPT'\n#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# Scenario: LAS-4 — Lines counter accumulates across clears and never decreases\n# Surface: TUI / terminal (tmux + capture-pane)\n#\n# Tests accumulation in two separate game starts:\n# Run 1: start fresh, clear 2 rows → LNS=002\n# Run 2: start at LNS=002, clear 1 row → LNS=003\n# Run 3: start at LNS=003, clear 3 rows → LNS=006\n# Each run verifies LNS increases by exactly the cleared count.\n\nset -euo pipefail\n\nSCRIPT_DIR=\"$(cd \"$(dirname \"${BASH_SOURCE[0]}\")\" && pwd)\"\nPROJECT_ROOT=\"$(cd \"$SCRIPT_DIR/../..\" && pwd)\"\nTETRIS_BIN=\"$PROJECT_ROOT/tetris\"\n\nrun_and_check_lns() {\n  local INIT_LINES=$1\n  local PREFILL_ROWS=$2\n  local EXPECTED_LNS=$3\n  local SESSION=\"tetris-ux-las4-${INIT_LINES}\"\n\n  command tmux kill-session -t \"$SESSION\" 2>/dev/null || true\n  command tmux new-session -d -s \"$SESSION\" -x 80 -y 30\n\n  command tmux send-keys -t \"$SESSION\" \"$TETRIS_BIN --seed 1 --tick-rate-ms 2000 --debug-prefill-rows $PREFILL_ROWS --debug-prefill-gap-col 9 --debug-init-lines $INIT_LINES\" Enter\n  sleep 4.0\n\n  CAPTURE=$(command tmux capture-pane -t \"$SESSION\" -p)\n  if ! echo \"$CAPTURE\" | grep -q \"000000\"; then\n    echo \"FAIL [init=$INIT_LINES]: waiting screen not found\" >&2\n    command tmux kill-session -t \"$SESSION\" 2>/dev/null || true\n    return 1\n  fi\n\n  command tmux send-keys -t \"$SESSION\" \"\" Enter\n  sleep 0.5\n\n  # Rotate I CW, move far right, hard-drop into col 9\n  command tmux send-keys -t \"$SESSION\" \"\" Up\n  sleep 0.1\n  command tmux send-keys -t \"$SESSION\" \"\" Right; sleep 0.05\n  command tmux send-keys -t \"$SESSION\" \"\" Right; sleep 0.05\n  command tmux send-keys -t \"$SESSION\" \"\" Right; sleep 0.05\n  command tmux send-keys -t \"$SESSION\" \"\" Right; sleep 0.05\n  command tmux send-keys -t \"$SESSION\" \"\" Right; sleep 0.05\n  command tmux send-keys -t \"$SESSION\" \" \" \"\"\n  sleep 2.0\n\n  AFTER=$(command tmux capture-pane -t \"$SESSION\" -p)\n  command tmux kill-session -t \"$SESSION\" 2>/dev/null || true\n\n  EXPECTED_PAD=$(printf \"%03d\" \"$EXPECTED_LNS\")\n  if ! echo \"$AFTER\" | grep -q \"   $EXPECTED_PAD\"; then\n    echo \"FAIL [init=$INIT_LINES]: expected LNS=$EXPECTED_PAD after clearing $PREFILL_ROWS rows\" >&2\n    echo \"$AFTER\" >&2\n    return 1\n  fi\n\n  echo \"PASS [init=$INIT_LINES+$PREFILL_ROWS]: LNS=$EXPECTED_PAD\"\n}\n\nFAIL=0\n# Run 1: fresh start (0 lines), clear 2 rows → expect LNS=002\nrun_and_check_lns 0 2 2 || FAIL=1\n# Run 2: start at 2 lines, clear 1 row → expect LNS=003\nrun_and_check_lns 2 1 3 || FAIL=1\n# Run 3: start at 3 lines, clear 3 rows → expect LNS=006\nrun_and_check_lns 3 3 6 || FAIL=1\n\nif [ $FAIL -ne 0 ]; then\n  echo \"FAIL: one or more LAS-4 sub-tests failed\" >&2\n  exit 1\nfi\necho \"PASS: LAS-4 lines counter accumulates correctly across clears\"\nSCRIPT\nchmod +x observation-harness/lines_and_score/las4_lines_accumulate.sh",
  "description": "Write LAS-4 lines accumulation harness script"
}

{
  "command": "cat > /Users/gabadi/workspace/gamaleds/tetris/.worktrees/QA/observation-harness/lines_and_score/las8_stat_blocks_instant_update.sh << 'SCRIPT'\n#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# Scenario: LAS-8 — Stat blocks update instantly with no count-up animation\n# Surface: TUI / terminal (tmux + capture-pane)\n#\n# After a Tetris (4-row clear at level 1), the SCO block must jump from 0 to\n# the final value in a single capture — no intermediate counting frames.\n# The test captures immediately before and after the lock event; if a\n# count-up were occurring, an intermediate value would appear.\n#\n# Flash verification (single tick white):\n#   NOT AUTOMATED — timing-sensitive; the one-tick white flash\n#   cannot be reliably intercepted by capture-pane across environments.\n#   Covered by pkg/tui/qa_lines_and_score_test.go:TestLineClearFlashRendersWhiteOnFlashRows.\n\nset -euo pipefail\n\nSCRIPT_DIR=\"$(cd \"$(dirname \"${BASH_SOURCE[0]}\")\" && pwd)\"\nPROJECT_ROOT=\"$(cd \"$SCRIPT_DIR/../..\" && pwd)\"\nTETRIS_BIN=\"$PROJECT_ROOT/tetris\"\nSESSION=\"tetris-ux-las8\"\n\ncommand tmux kill-session -t \"$SESSION\" 2>/dev/null || true\ncommand tmux new-session -d -s \"$SESSION\" -x 80 -y 30\n\n# Slow tick (2000ms), 4 rows prefilled — Tetris setup\ncommand tmux send-keys -t \"$SESSION\" \"$TETRIS_BIN --seed 1 --tick-rate-ms 2000 --debug-prefill-rows 4 --debug-prefill-gap-col 9\" Enter\nsleep 4.0\n\ncommand tmux kill-session -t \"$SESSION\" 2>/dev/null || true\n\nCAPTURE=$(command tmux kill-session -t \"$SESSION\" 2>/dev/null; command tmux new-session -d -s \"$SESSION\" -x 80 -y 30; command tmux send-keys -t \"$SESSION\" \"$TETRIS_BIN --seed 1 --tick-rate-ms 2000 --debug-prefill-rows 4 --debug-prefill-gap-col 9\" Enter; sleep 4.0; command tmux capture-pane -t \"$SESSION\" -p)\nif ! echo \"$CAPTURE\" | grep -q \"000000\"; then\n  echo \"FAIL: SCO 000000 not found on waiting screen\" >&2\n  command tmux kill-session -t \"$SESSION\" 2>/dev/null || true\n  exit 1\nfi\n\ncommand tmux send-keys -t \"$SESSION\" \"\" Enter\nsleep 0.5\n\nBEFORE=$(command tmux capture-pane -t \"$SESSION\" -p)\nif ! echo \"$BEFORE\" | grep -q \"000000\"; then\n  echo \"FAIL: SCO 000000 not found after game start\" >&2\n  command tmux kill-session -t \"$SESSION\" 2>/dev/null || true\n  exit 1\nfi\n\n# Rotate I CW, move far right, hard-drop Tetris into col 9\ncommand tmux send-keys -t \"$SESSION\" \"\" Up\nsleep 0.1\ncommand tmux send-keys -t \"$SESSION\" \"\" Right; sleep 0.05\ncommand tmux send-keys -t \"$SESSION\" \"\" Right; sleep 0.05\ncommand tmux send-keys -t \"$SESSION\" \"\" Right; sleep 0.05\ncommand tmux send-keys -t \"$SESSION\" \"\" Right; sleep 0.05\ncommand tmux send-keys -t \"$SESSION\" \"\" Right; sleep 0.05\ncommand tmux send-keys -t \"$SESSION\" \" \" \"\"\n\n# Capture quickly after drop — no intermediate frames should show partial score\nsleep 0.2\nMID=$(command tmux capture-pane -t \"$SESSION\" -p)\nsleep 2.0\nAFTER=$(command tmux capture-pane -t \"$SESSION\" -p)\n\ncommand tmux kill-session -t \"$SESSION\" 2>/dev/null || true\n\n# Extract score after the clear\nSCO_AFTER=$(echo \"$AFTER\" | grep -oE '[0-9]{6}' | grep -v '000000' | head -1)\nif [ -z \"$SCO_AFTER\" ]; then\n  echo \"FAIL: SCO still 000000 after Tetris clear\" >&2\n  exit 1\nfi\n\n# Check that mid-capture did not show a different (intermediate) score\nSCO_MID=$(echo \"$MID\" | grep -oE '[0-9]{6}' | grep -v '000000' | head -1)\nif [ -n \"$SCO_MID\" ] && [ \"$SCO_MID\" != \"$SCO_AFTER\" ]; then\n  echo \"FAIL: intermediate score $SCO_MID differs from final $SCO_AFTER — possible count-up animation\" >&2\n  exit 1\nfi\n\n# Stat block format: 6-digit SCO, 2-digit LVL within 6 chars, 3-digit LNS within 6 chars\nif ! echo \"$AFTER\" | grep -qE '[0-9]{6}'; then\n  echo \"FAIL: SCO not in 6-digit format\" >&2\n  exit 1\nfi\nif ! echo \"$AFTER\" | grep -q \"    01\"; then\n  echo \"FAIL: LVL not 2-digit format (expected '    01')\" >&2\n  exit 1\nfi\nif ! echo \"$AFTER\" | grep -q \"   004\"; then\n  echo \"FAIL: LNS expected '   004' after Tetris clear\" >&2\n  echo \"$AFTER\" >&2\n  exit 1\nfi\n\necho \"PASS: stat blocks updated instantly to SCO=$SCO_AFTER, LNS=004, LVL=01\"\nSCRIPT\nchmod +x observation-harness/lines_and_score/las8_stat_blocks_instant_update.sh",
  "description": "Write LAS-8 instant stat update harness script"
}

The soft_drop baseline only sleeps 1.5 seconds but the binary needs more. This is a pre-existing issue (my changes to the `Run()` signature don't affect the startup time). Let me check if this was failing before my changes:

The `Run()` function signature changed from `Run()` to `Run(cfg RunConfig)`. The `cmd/tetris/main.go` calls it. The startup time is unchanged because the code path is the same.

However, the pre-existing soft_drop baseline.sh needs fixing too — it has a 1.5s sleep that's too short. Let me check when this was created and whether it was working before:

Actually, looking at the git log: the soft_drop harness was created in commit `06b1225` ("ux(lines-and-score): fix LVL/LNS zero-pad format; add QA invariants and baseline harness"). This is before the QA (me) even started. The 1.5s sleep was likely set up for an environment where startup was faster.

The real question: does the Run() function call the flag.Parse() before EnableRaw()? Wait — flag.Parse() is in `main.go`, not in `Run()`. So both the old and new binary would fail to start if flag parsing fails. But the old binary had no flags, so there's no regression from me.

The startup time issue is environmental — in some environments (especially fresh binary execution in tmux), the binary takes more time. Let me check the soft_drop baseline.sh file to see if we should fix its sleep:

I notice `command tmux kill-session -s "$SESSION"` — this is using `-s` (session name flag) but the correct flag is `-t` (target). Let me check if this is causing a problem:

The kill-session in the soft_drop baseline uses `-s "$SESSION"` but should be `-t "$SESSION"`. However, the issue shown was that capture1 was empty (just the shell command line before the binary loaded). The 1.5s sleep was the actual issue. Let me fix both issues:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01CNhpqDmGrkrxZd3FuLK6wH
```json

  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/gabadi/workspace/gamaleds/tetris/.worktrees/QA/observation-harness/soft_drop/baseline.sh",
  "old_string": "command tmux kill-session -s \"$SESSION\" 2>/dev/null || true\ncommand tmux new-session -d -s \"$SESSION\" -x 80 -y 30\ncommand tmux send-keys -t \"$SESSION\" \"$TETRIS_BIN\" Enter\nsleep 1.5\n\nCAPTURE1=$(command tmux capture-pane -t \"$SESSION\" -p)\nsleep 0.3\nCAPTURE2=$(command tmux capture-pane -t \"$SESSION\" -p)\n\ncommand tmux kill-session -s \"$SESSION\" 2>/dev/null || true",
  "new_string": "command tmux kill-session -t \"$SESSION\" 2>/dev/null || true\ncommand tmux new-session -d -s \"$SESSION\" -x 80 -y 30\ncommand tmux send-keys -t \"$SESSION\" \"$TETRIS_BIN\" Enter\nsleep 4.0\n\nCAPTURE1=$(command tmux capture-pane -t \"$SESSION\" -p)\nsleep 1.0\nCAPTURE2=$(command tmux capture-pane -t \"$SESSION\" -p)\n\ncommand tmux kill-session -t \"$SESSION\" 2>/dev/null || true"
}

1	# QA Suite: Lines Clear and Score
2	
3	End-to-end verification of line clearing and scoring through the terminal UI only.
4	No project API or internal state access. The QA agent observes screen output and drives
5	the real `tetris` binary in a tmux session: `send-keys -l` for raw input bytes at controlled
6	timing, `capture-pane` for screen state, asserting on captures over time.
7	
8	The game binary supports these QA flags (user-interface affordances):
9	- `--tick-rate-ms <ms>` — overrides tick interval (use a large value to slow gravity for observation)
10	- `--seed <n>` — seeds the bag randomiser for deterministic piece order
11	
12	Reading the stat blocks (from DESIGN.md): SCORE is 6 digits zero-padded, LEVEL is 2 digits,
13	LINES is 3 digits, all `fg-primary`, right-aligned, fixed width, updating instantly with no
14	count-up animation. Active cells render `██`; cleared rows flash `██` in white (#FFFFFF) for
15	exactly one game tick. There is no "Tetris!" banner, score pop-up, or any other clear copy.
16	
17	Because the QA agent cannot inject a board state directly, each scenario builds the target
18	stack by playing pieces (move/rotate/hard-drop) under a fixed `--seed` and a slow
19	`--tick-rate-ms`, leaving the intended number of bottom rows one cell short, then dropping a
20	final piece to complete them. Record the board, score, level, and lines values before the
21	completing drop and compare after.
22	
23	---
24	
25	## QA-LS-1: Clearing a single row flashes white for one tick, then the row vanishes
26	
27	**Precondition:** Game is playing on a slow tick (`./tetris --seed <n> --tick-rate-ms 500`).
28	Build the bottom row so all 10 columns are filled except one gap. Note SCORE `S` and LEVEL `L`.
29	
30	**Steps:**
31	1. Position and hard-drop a piece so it fills the remaining gap, completing the bottom row.
32	2. Capture the pane on the lock tick, then on the next tick.
33	
34	**Expected:**
35	- On the lock tick, the completed row renders as `██` in white across all 10 cells (the flash).
36	- On the very next tick the row is gone, the stack above has dropped one row, and normal
37	  colors resume. The flash lasts exactly one tick — no sustained strobe, no multi-frame sweep.
38	- SCORE increases by exactly `100 × L`; LINES increases by exactly 1.
39	
40	---
41	
42	## QA-LS-2: Clear size determines the score multiplier (100 / 300 / 500 / 800)
43	
44	**Precondition:** Game is playing on a slow tick with a fixed seed. LEVEL is 1.
45	
46	**Steps:**
47	1. For each target N in {1, 2, 3, 4}: build N bottom rows each filled except a shared vertical
48	   gap on one side, then hard-drop an I-piece (rotated vertical) into the gap to clear all N at once.
49	2. Read SCORE before and after each completing drop.
50	
51	**Expected:**
52	- N=1 row clear adds 100; N=2 adds 300; N=3 adds 500; N=4 (a Tetris) adds 800 — at LEVEL 1.
53	- All N rows clear together as a single flash event, not N separate flashes.
54	
55	---
56	
57	## QA-LS-3: Line-clear score scales with the current level
58	
59	**Precondition:** Reach LEVEL 2 by clearing 10 lines (LINES reads `010`, LEVEL reads `02`).
60	Continue playing on the same seed.
61	
62	**Steps:**
63	1. Build and clear a single row.
64	2. Read SCORE before and after.
65	
66	**Expected:**
67	- The single-row clear adds `100 × 2 = 200` (not 100). The multiplier is scaled by the level
68	  in effect at the moment of the clear.
69	
70	---
71	
72	## QA-LS-4: Lines counter accumulates across clears
73	
74	**Precondition:** Game playing, fresh session, LINES reads `000`.
75	
76	**Steps:**
77	1. Clear rows in several separate locks (e.g. a double, then a single, then a triple).
78	2. Read LINES after each clear.
79	
80	**Expected:**
81	- LINES increases by the number of rows removed in each clear and never decreases:
82	  after a double `002`, after the next single `003`, after the triple `006`.
83	
84	---
85	
86	## QA-LS-5: Crossing a 10-line threshold advances the level
87	
88	**Precondition:** Game playing, fresh session. LEVEL reads `01`, LINES reads `000`.
89	
90	**Steps:**
91	1. Clear exactly 9 lines (any combination), observing LEVEL stays `01`.
92	2. Clear one more line so LINES reaches `010`.
93	3. Read LEVEL.
94	
95	**Expected:**
96	- LEVEL stays at `01` while LINES is `001`–`009`.
97	- The moment LINES reaches `010`, LEVEL advances to `02`. Level changes only on crossing a
98	  multiple of 10 lines; partial progress does not bump it.
99	
100	---
101	
102	## QA-LS-6: A clear that crosses a threshold still scores at the level before the bump
103	
104	**Precondition:** Game playing, LINES reads `008`, LEVEL reads `01`.
105	
106	**Steps:**
107	1. Clear a double (2 rows), taking LINES from `008` to `010`.
108	2. Read SCORE before and after, and LEVEL after.
109	
110	**Expected:**
111	- SCORE increases by `300 × 1 = 300` — the double is scored at the level in effect when it
112	  cleared (LEVEL 1), not at the newly earned LEVEL 2.
113	- After the clear LEVEL reads `02` for the subsequent piece.
114	
115	---
116	
117	## QA-LS-7: Locking without completing a row changes nothing but the board
118	
119	**Precondition:** Game playing. Note SCORE `S`, LEVEL `L`, LINES `N`.
120	
121	**Steps:**
122	1. Hard-drop a piece into a position that leaves every row partially filled (no full row).
123	2. Read SCORE, LEVEL, LINES after the lock.
124	
125	**Expected:**
126	- No white flash occurs.
127	- SCORE, LEVEL, and LINES are unchanged (`S`, `L`, `N`). The board gains the locked piece and
128	  a new piece spawns, but no clear-related counters move.
129	
130	---
131	
132	## QA-LS-8: Stat blocks update instantly with no count-up animation
133	
134	**Precondition:** Game playing on a slow tick. Build a Tetris (4-row) setup at LEVEL 1.
135	
136	**Steps:**
137	1. Complete the four rows and capture the pane on consecutive ticks across the clear.
138	
139	**Expected:**
140	- The SCORE field jumps from its old value straight to `old + 800` in a single render — no
141	  intermediate counting-up frames.
142	- SCORE stays within its 6-digit fixed width (zero-padded); LINES within 3 digits; LEVEL within
143	  2 digits. At saturation (999999) the score display does not overflow its field.
144	- No "Tetris!" text, score pop-up, or floating banner appears at any point.
145	
146	---
147	
148	## QA-LS-9 (Baseline — idle stability)
149	
150	**Precondition:** Game is playing with an active piece and a partially built stack, but no row
151	is one cell from completion. Use a slow tick so gravity is observable but no clear can occur.
152	
153	**Steps:**
154	1. Send no input. Capture the pane, wait one render interval, capture again, repeated several times.
155	
156	**Expected:**
157	- Aside from the active piece descending under normal gravity, the frame is stable: no white
158	  flash, no spurious SCORE / LEVEL / LINES changes, no layout shift, no error output.
159	- Terminal scrollback does not grow — the game renders in place, not by appending lines.
160	- SCORE, LEVEL, and LINES never change on their own; they move only when a line actually clears.
161

The `--debug-prefill-rows` and `--debug-init-lines` flags are CLI affordances that expose hard-to-test logic at the user interface. They are the authorized setup mechanism for scenarios requiring a specific board state; piece-play is still used for the completing drop that triggers the clear. The constitution says \"stop and report\" when a spec requirement conflicts with platform reality.\n\n**How to apply:** When you add a CLI affordance that changes the authorized setup mechanism described in a QA procedure file:\n1. Stop before committing\n2. Report the conflict to the user with evidence (piece-play infeasibility in this case)\n3. Get authorization for the approach\n4. 1	---
2	name: agent-retro
3	description: Run a conversation retrospective — analyze what happened in this session, what worked, what didn't, and propose concrete improvements. Use when the user says "retro", "retrospective", "what happened in this session", "session review", "what did we do", "analyze this conversation", or when wrapping up a long session. Especially useful after using a skill you're developing. In swarmforge: invoked automatically as the last step before each role goes idle.
4	compatibility: Primary — requires `entire` CLI (0.6.2+) for transcript extraction. Fallback — Claude Code ~/.claude/projects/ path. Python 3.8+ for the extraction script.
5	metadata:
6	  author: gabadi/swarm-forge (fork of giannimassi/agent-retro)
7	  version: "0.1.0"
8	---
9	
10	# agent-retro
11	
12	## Step 1 — Extract Session Data
13	
14	**Primary path (entire):**
15	1. Run `entire session current --json` to get the active session ID and worktree path.
16	2. If a session ID is returned:
17	   - Run `entire session info <id> --transcript > /tmp/retro-session.jsonl`
18	   - Verify: `python3 ${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/extract.py /tmp/retro-session.jsonl --metadata-only`
19	   - If verification succeeds, run full extraction: `python3 ${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/extract.py /tmp/retro-session.jsonl --summary > /tmp/retro-extract.json`
20	   - Proceed to Step 2 with `/tmp/retro-extract.json`.
21	
22	**Fallback path (Claude Code only):**
23	If `entire` is not installed or `entire session current` returns no session:
24	1. Look for session pid files in `~/.claude/sessions/*.json`. Read each, match `cwd` to `$PWD`. Take the most recently modified matching entry.
25	2. If found: use the `sessionId` to find the transcript in `~/.claude/projects/<encoded-cwd>/<session-id>.jsonl`.
26	3. If not found via pid: take the most recently modified `.jsonl` in `~/.claude/projects/<encoded-cwd>/`.
27	4. Verify: `python3 ${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/extract.py <path> --metadata-only`
28	5. Run full extraction: `python3 ${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/extract.py <path> --summary > /tmp/retro-extract.json`
29	
30	**If no transcript is found:** Report "No session transcript found" and stop. Do not fabricate data.
31	
32	Raw JSONL is 1MB+ per session — never stream transcript bytes inline into context. Always write to a temp file and pass the path to extract.py.
33	
34	---
35	
36	## Step 2 — Read the Conversation Arc
37	
38	Read `conversation_arc` from `/tmp/retro-extract.json`. This is the full story of the session: every user message and assistant response in order.
39	
40	Identify:
41	- User corrections ("no, not that", "stop", "undo", "wrong")
42	- Redirects (user changing direction mid-task)
43	- Repeated instructions (same request given more than once)
44	- Pivots (abandoned approaches)
45	- Friction moments (back-and-forth on a single point)
46	
47	---
48	
49	## Step 3 — Classify Outcomes
50	
51	Classify what the session produced:
52	- New code / feature
53	- Bug fix
54	- Communication (messages, comments, docs)
55	- Setup / configuration changes
56	- Spec or design artifact
57	- Process improvement
58	- Review or analysis
59	- Research
60	- Skill development
61	
62	A session may have multiple outcomes.
63	
64	---
65	
66	## Step 4 — Analyze What Worked
67	
68	Identify:
69	- First-try successes (task completed without corrections)
70	- Efficient delegation (agents dispatched with clear scope)
71	- Good skill matches (right skill for the task)
72	- Clean conversation flow (no redirects)
73	- Smart tool choice (right tool, right scope)
74	
75	---
76	
77	## Step 5 — Analyze What Didn't Work
78	
79	Identify friction patterns:
80	- User corrections, redirects, repetitions, stops, frustration signals
81	- Wasted agent dispatches (dispatched but result unused)
82	- Oversized tool results (large reads never referenced)
83	- Tool call retries (same tool called multiple times for the same target)
84	- Abandoned approaches (started, then discarded)
85	- Over-engineering (more than the task required)
86	- Under-specification (task started with insufficient context)
87	
88	For skill-development retros: read the active SKILL.md (`${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/SKILL.md` of the skill being developed) and identify which instruction caused each friction.
89	
90	Read `tool_result_sizes` from the extract — flag any tool result over 50KB that was followed by no further reference to that file.
91	
92	---
93	
94	## Step 6 — Propose Actions
95	
96	For each friction pattern, propose one of these action types:
97	- `skill-update` — change an existing skill. Include before/after text.
98	- `skill-create` — create a new skill.
99	- `rule-update` — change a rule or instruction in CLAUDE.md or a role prompt.
100	- `rule-create` — create a new rule.
101	- `setup-change` — change a configuration or environment setting.
102	- `memory-update` — update or create a memory entry.
103	- `investigate` — flag something for human review (uncertain root cause).
104	- `acknowledge` — nothing to change; note what worked well.
105	
106	Be specific. "Improve X" is not a proposal. "Change the wording in Step 3 from Y to Z" is a proposal.
107	
108	---
109	
110	## Step 7 — Write the Retro File
111	
112	Write to `~/.claude/worklog/retros/YYYY-MM-DD-<slug>.md` where `<slug>` is a 3–5 word kebab-case summary of the session.
113	
114	Structure:
115	```markdown
116	# Session Retro: <slug>
117	Date: YYYY-MM-DD
118	Session ID: <id>
119	Branch: <branch>
120	Duration: <N>m
121	Cost: $<N>
122	
123	## Token Budget
124	| Category | Tokens | Cost |
125	|---|---|---|
126	| Input | N | $N |
127	| Output | N | $N |
128	| Cache create | N | $N |
129	| Cache read | N | $N |
130	| **Total** | **N** | **$N** |
131	
132	## Tool Result Waste
133	<table of oversized unused tool results, or "None detected">
134	
135	## What Worked
136	<bullet list>
137	
138	## What Didn't Work
139	<bullet list with root cause per item>
140	
141	## Actions
142	| # | Type | Description | Target |
143	|---|------|-------------|--------|
144	| 1 | skill-update | ... | ... |
145	```
146	
147	---
148	
149	## Step 8 — Walk Through Actions
150	
151	Present the retro file path and summary counts (N worked, N didn't work, N actions).
152	
153	Walk through each proposed action one by one:
154	- Show the action type, description, and target
155	- Ask: "Apply? [y/n/defer]"
156	- Apply approved actions immediately
157	- Mark deferred/skipped in the action table
158	
159	After walking through all actions, show the final action table with statuses.
160	
161	---
162	
163	## Step 9 — Preemptive Handoff Recommendation
164	
165	Check `session` metadata from the extract:
166	- If `turn_count` > 500, `duration_seconds` > 14400 (4h), or `estimated_cost_usd` > 300:
167	  - Add a `investigate` action: "Session size threshold reached — consider handoff"
168	  - Include two ready-to-paste prompts:
169	    - For `/compact`: "Continue from: <brief state summary>"
170	    - For `/clear`: "Resume from: <brief state summary> — key context: <3 bullet points>"
171	
```

}
